Pearson Edexcel Mathematics Mock Questions

International GCSE · International A Level · Calculation-rich practice

20 chapters · 70 questions per chapter · 1,400 question cards · 100 embedded diagrams

Choose the correct qualification route

Important: This is a CBE Nexus learning-resource sequence, not a verbatim Pearson specification or an official Pearson examination paper. International GCSE Mathematics A linear (4MA1) and modular (4XMA1) are separate routes; the chapter sequence below organises practice topics for both, but the assessment structure and unit entry must follow the centre's registered specification. International A Level Mathematics has compulsory Pure Mathematics units P1–P4 and applied-unit choices. Mechanics and Statistics are shown as separate applied topics and must be matched to the candidate's actual unit combination.

International GCSE: use the first ten chapters for numerical, algebraic, graphical, geometric, statistical and probability practice. Select Foundation or Higher demand according to the entered tier. For modular candidates, organise revision by the registered module and specification.

International A Level: use chapters 11–16 for Pure Mathematics content and chapters 17–20 for applied Mechanics and Statistics. These topic chapters are study groupings; they do not replace Pearson's official unit boundaries or option rules.

Chapter index

1. Number and numerical reasoning2. Algebraic manipulation and proof3. Equations, inequalities and formulae4. Graphs, functions and transformations5. Sequences and series6. Geometry, constructions and vectors7. Mensuration and circle geometry8. Trigonometry and bearings9. Statistics and data representation10. Probability and statistical reasoning11. Pure Mathematics: algebra and functions12. Pure Mathematics: coordinate geometry13. Pure Mathematics: trigonometry14. Pure Mathematics: differentiation15. Pure Mathematics: integration16. Pure Mathematics: exponentials and logarithms17. Mechanics: modelling, kinematics and forces18. Statistics: sampling and data presentation19. Statistics: probability distributions20. Statistics: hypothesis testing

All questions are CBE Nexus practice questions, not official past-paper questions. For each question, show full working, include units, state assumptions and round only as instructed. Diagrams are schematic and not necessarily to scale.

1. Number and numerical reasoning

Coverage: Integers, fractions, decimals, percentages, ratio, standard form, bounds, surds and numerical accuracy.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 1

Worked example 1. A laboratory records 0.000684 kg. Express this in standard form in grams.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

70 examination practice questions

Questions include calculation, multi-step reasoning, interpretation, proof and application. Show full working and give units where appropriate.

QUESTION 01DIAGRAM · CALCULATION / REASONING · 4 MARKS

A laboratory records 0.000684 kg. Express this in standard form in grams. Include units and an appropriate degree of accuracy.

QUESTION 02DIAGRAM · CALCULATION / REASONING · 5 MARKS

A hiking route is 18.6 km. A map uses scale 1:25 000. Calculate the map distance in cm. Explain why the method is valid and identify any restrictions.

QUESTION 03DIAGRAM · CALCULATION / REASONING · 6 MARKS

A machine produces 1,260 components, of which 3.5% are defective. Find the number accepted. Show all intermediate calculations; unsupported answers receive no method credit.

QUESTION 04DIAGRAM · CALCULATION / REASONING · 7 MARKS

A tank is 7/12 full. After 35 L is added it is 5/6 full. Find its capacity. Interpret the result in the context of the problem.

QUESTION 05DIAGRAM · CALCULATION / REASONING · 3 MARKS

A length is 8.42 cm correct to 0.01 cm. State its error interval and percentage error. Check your result using an alternative method or substitution.

QUESTION 06CALCULATION · REASONING · 4 MARKS

2. Algebraic manipulation and proof

Coverage: Expressions, identities, factorisation, algebraic fractions, indices, surds and proof.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 2

Worked example 1. Expand and simplify (3x−5)(2x+7)−(x−4)².
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 4. Simplify (x²−9)/(x²+5x+6), stating all excluded values.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

3. Equations, inequalities and formulae

Coverage: Linear and quadratic equations, simultaneous equations, inequalities, rearrangement and modelling.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 3

Worked example 1. Solve 3(2x−5)−4(x+1)=17 and verify the result.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 4

Worked example 1. For f(x)=x²−4x−5, find the intercepts, turning point and axis of symmetry.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

5. Sequences and series

Coverage: Arithmetic and geometric sequences, nth terms, sums, recurrence and applications.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 5

Worked example 1. An arithmetic sequence has u₄=17 and u₁₁=52. Find its first term, common difference and u₃₀.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

8. Trigonometry and bearings

Coverage: Right-triangle trigonometry, sine/cosine rules, radians, graphs, identities and bearings.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 8

Worked example 1. From a point 48 m from a tower, the angle of elevation is 37°. Calculate the tower height if the observer's eye is 1.6 m above ground.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 2. A ship sails 18 km on a bearing of 065°, then 24 km on a bearing of 155°. Calculate its distance and bearing from the start.

  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

9. Statistics and data representation

Coverage: Sampling, grouped data, cumulative frequency, histograms, averages, spread and interpretation.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 9

Worked example 1. Grouped travel-time data have classes 0–10, 10–20, 20–40, 40–70 with frequencies 8, 17, 21, 14. Estimate the mean.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

10. Probability and statistical reasoning

Coverage: Conditional probability, tree diagrams, independent events, distributions and expected value.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 10

Worked example 1. A bag contains 5 red, 4 blue and 3 green counters. Two are drawn without replacement. Find the probability they are different colours.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 2. Events A and B have P(A)=0.6, P(B)=0.45 and P(A∩B)=0.27. Test independence and calculate P(A|B).
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.

  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 4. A game costs £3 to play. A fair six-sided die pays £0, £2, £5 or £12 depending on the score. Determine the expected net gain and whether the game is fair.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

11. Pure Mathematics: algebra and functions

Coverage: Advanced algebra, polynomial functions, partial fractions, inequalities, modulus and mathematical proof.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 11

Worked example 1. Resolve (5x+7)/((x−1)(x+2)) into partial fractions and state the domain.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

14. Pure Mathematics: differentiation

Coverage: Differentiation, chain/product/quotient rules, implicit differentiation, tangents and optimisation.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 14

Worked example 1. Differentiate y=(2x³−5x+1)(x²+4) and evaluate dy/dx at x=1.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

15. Pure Mathematics: integration

Coverage: Indefinite and definite integration, substitution, parts, areas and differential equations.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 15

Worked example 1. Evaluate ∫(6x²−4x+3/x²) dx and use F(1)=5 to find the constant.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

16. Pure Mathematics: exponentials and logarithms

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 16

Worked example 1. Solve 5^(2x−1)=17, giving x to 4 decimal places.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

18. Statistics: sampling and data presentation

Coverage: Sampling methods, data collection, measures, coding, grouped data and graphical presentation.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 18

Worked example 1. A sample of 80 batteries has mean life 412 hours and standard deviation 36 hours. Estimate the standard error of the mean.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

19. Statistics: probability distributions

Coverage: Discrete and continuous random variables, binomial and normal distributions, expectation and variance.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 19

Worked example 1. X~B(18,0.3). Find P(X=4), P(X≥7), E(X) and Var(X).
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

20. Statistics: hypothesis testing

Coverage: Null and alternative hypotheses, critical regions, p-values, one- and two-tailed tests and interpretation.

Five worked-method examples — Chapter 20

Worked example 1. A coin shows 17 heads in 25 tosses. Test at 5% whether it is biased, stating H₀, H₁ and the test statistic.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 2. A manufacturer claims mean fill is 330 ml. A sample of 36 has mean 326.8 ml and known σ=9 ml. Carry out a 1% lower-tail test.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 3. In 80 trials an event occurs 54 times. Test H₀:p=0.6 against H₁:p≠0.6 at 5%, using a suitable approximation.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.
  3. Substitute: Insert the given values carefully, retaining exact values and units where possible.
  4. Calculate: Show each rearrangement and intermediate step; do not round until the final stage.
  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 4. Explain the difference between a Type I and Type II error in a medical screening context, and identify the consequence of each.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
  2. Choose a method: Select the appropriate formula, theorem, algebraic operation or statistical procedure for this topic.

  5. Check and conclude: Check the result by substitution, estimation or an alternative method. State the answer with units and suitable accuracy.

Examiner focus: method, accurate working, interpretation and a clearly stated conclusion.

Worked example 5. A test gives p=0.032. Interpret the result at 5% and 1% significance levels without claiming that H₀ is the probability of being true.
  1. Identify: Write down the known quantities, unknown quantity and the mathematical relationship required.
